ParlaMint-AT_1996-03-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00010_d7e3359	Mr President! Mr President of the National Council! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! As the elected representatives of the Austrian people, I present to you today the new Federal Government and its programme for the next legislative period. I do this in a good tradition of cooperation between legislation and administration, between Parliament and the Federal Government. At the beginning of the XX. I invite all of you to cooperate with the Austrian Federal Government in the legislative period of the Second Republic, which is to lead us until the next millennium. Ladies and gentlemen! The tasks and challenges we face are anything but easy. When I speak of a good tradition, I mean the starting point, because not everything that is tradition can be applied for the future and therefore cannot always be called "good". The conflict, the escalation, the fight will not be spared us, and we must not spare ourselves either. But if we were not to find solutions afterwards, we would have disputed for nothing and would rightly be punished. The Social Democratic Party of Austria was seen as the strongest and strongest party in the election in December 1995. As chairman of this party, the President of the Federal Government has entrusted me with the formation of the government. It is now completed together with the Austrian People's Party. Some may appear to be two and a half months for government formation too long, all the more so than a federal budget for 1996. However, I can and will report to the House that, at this time, it has come too much more than the creation of a new federal government and the creation of a very extensive work programme. For the first time in the Second Republic, Parliament will be presented with a budget for the current but also for the next year. In this way, the new Federal Government not only ensures the consolidation of Austrian state finances in the short term, but also in the medium term. We have therefore done a lot more in the last few weeks than just negotiating a government compilation. We have completed the salary round with the public service, the financial compensatory negotiations with the federal states and municipalities are a positive one and we have started numerous structural reforms. After intensive working days and weeks, we are at the beginning of an equally intensive legislative period. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! Social Democrats and People's Party interpret the results of the election of 17th century. In December 1995 as the wish of the Austrian population, both parties have to commit themselves for the full four years to deal with the upcoming issues in our country. All members of the Federal Government make this commitment, also in the knowledge and confidence of a similar cooperation here in the House. This shows not the weakness, but the strength of Austrian parliamentarianism. Without Parliament, a government in Austria cannot, in any case, rule wisely! [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] The two parties presented their ideas and demands to voters in the election movement. In the negotiations, they have consistently put together the most important demands from the election campaign, settled against each other and found a compromise. Now the voters have the right to fully and quickly implement this compromise. The search for a majority in individual cases is diametrically opposed to this desire. I recognize the cooperation of these two parties, not just from the perspective of political arithmetic. It has succeeded in making adjustments without giving up principles and involving the social partners, other stakeholders and the local authorities, which would certainly not have been achieved in another form of political cooperation. The inevitable question about the winner has already been asked. The answer is clear. The winner is our country, which will once again receive a calculated and stable state government that rests on a good foundation. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] However, another reason is very important to me why the cooperation between SPÖ and ÖVP is the best for our country under the given circumstances. At a time when radicalisms are no longer just exotic marginalities, at a time when politically motivated terrorist attacks are also practiced in our country, we are dealing with civilized and predictable ways of dealing with politics and public life. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! At such a time, it is also about moderation and consideration in political reasoning and language. In such a time, interconnecting is more important than interconnecting and interplaying. Austria needs – I mean – a government that makes consensus its maximum of action and does not strengthen polarization. [[New applause with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] High house! A declaration of government must first and foremost implement the principle of political position. Since this Federal Government is to lead Austria up to the magic year 2000, there should be fundamental considerations at the beginning of its work, in which direction our country is heading and which cliffs it is to be bypassed. Our world changes at an unprecedented speed and to an unprecedented extent. That policy is called for by politicians seems to me of course. The times have become more crisis-like, more complex and inconspicuous than ever before. It is claimed that, in view of this, politics and politicians have become more insolent. It's not like that, I mean. They will only increasingly become a kind of "universal responsibility" as expressed by German sociologist Niklas Luhmann; "universal responsibility" who must have prepared answers as soon as possible, as far as possible, generally and as much as possible. These demands are not satisfied by anyone, nor by politicians. We must therefore seek and provide political solutions with honesty, openness and willingness to cooperate. The political image of the future will no longer be able to be exclusive, to provide apartments and loans, to guarantee jobs and to pay business rounds. The policy of the future will be to give people a guide, to open up to them the possibilities of developing themselves out of a good economic and social security, to perceive their own opportunities, to take their fate into their own hands, but to know, in any case, that they are not alone. That is how we must understand our community, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] The progressive division of work leads to a stronger internal distinction in society. Large operating units are more and more broken down into small units. Increasingly large proportions of production are ordered from subcontractors. Or: Women with Matura – 20 years ago in minority numbers – are now an important group. In another twenty years, they will be more than all auxiliary workers together. Or: The traditional pattern of life by which one makes a training in youth, in which one learns everything necessary for the profession, this pattern of life becomes increasingly rare. It is replaced by life-long learning. Knowledge and information are produced, distributed and sold faster, more extensive, more diversified than with the old economic patterns of explanation. Ladies and gentlemen! These examples could continue. These are new social patterns that also demand new instruments of policy making. The state is no longer responsible and responsible for all and all. But he has to take care of the basic safeguards. He has the infrastructure available, i.e. roads, railways, schools, hospitals, telecommunications. This costs money and costs more and more money in view of the greater opportunities for education, longer life and better health. For this reason, responsible policies will always have to go into the field of alternatives and find themselves there in a marked way. This also includes, and above all, preparing the economic foundations in such a way as to ensure future-oriented jobs and thus consumption and taxation. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] High house! We know – and we live at a time when unemployment has again become one of the most pressing problems of our society; worldwide, more than 800 million people today are without work – that the upturn of the world economy will not be enough to eliminate or end this global mass unemployment. We will therefore have to work again and again on how to deal with the problem of underemployment and overproductivity and what position our Austrian and our employees will have in a global economy. In any case, it is one of the major aims of the Federal Government to expand Austria's location and secure employment. Austria has highly qualified workers. Austria has a lot of creativity, commitment and diligence. We need to benefit from this together, and that is why the Federal Government has launched a new offensive for growth and employment. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] If the German Gunter Hofmann wrote recently: Today, the conditions dictate the laws, then we deliberately do not find ourselves with this sentence. Ladies and gentlemen! It is five major objectives that the new Federal Government has committed itself to its work, five major objectives to be pursued over the next four years. In addition to the employment offensive and the expansion of Austria's economic location, it will be a consolidated government budget. In doing so, we do not understand savings as a self-evident, but as a necessary prerequisite for having room for movement and to remain politically operational. The third major political area to which the new Federal Government will devote itself is Austria's role as a member of the European Union. Before the start of the Intergovernmental Conference this year, two years before the Austrian Presidency in the European Union, but also before the first EU election in Austria this year, new impetus needs to be set in order to be even stronger and better informed and co-formed than before. A fourth major field of policy is that of security. In addition to internal and external security, we are based on a comprehensive concept of security which also addresses social and environmental security. Austria has taken on a pioneering role both in environmental and social standards. Finally, fifthly, we want to devote a number of reform measures to very different areas, such as training, justice, health, transport and many other issues. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] High house! This programme is an offensive reform programme in all areas of society. We're writing the facts, the states aren't just going on. The public expenditure and the tasks of the state have been reviewed for their purpose. They have been evaluated quantitatively and qualitatively, and this has resulted in a very courageous programme because it covers many so-called taboo themes. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] High house! Long before its formal education, the Federal Government, together with the social partners, launched an offensive for growth and employment. Although our economic data are significantly better than most EU Member States, we must not wait until the crisis reaches Austria. This task is serious. Even economic growth does not necessarily lead to a reduction in unemployment. To guarantee jobs and finance them through the budget is not a common way. It is necessary to have an economic policy that makes it possible to expand and build up existing businesses – whether by foreigners or by foreigners – and thus to create or maintain meaningful jobs. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Since the export potential of the Austrian economy is not exhausted, the presence on foreign markets must be supported, industrial settlements must be promoted and access to capital should be facilitated for small and medium-sized export-intensive companies. Investments in transport, environment, energy and telecommunications must be strengthened. They not only improve the quality of life of citizens, but above all also the quality of the economic location. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! In addition, we have planned to increase housing aid, to facilitate environmental investments and building renovations in order to help the economy in this way. And because the economy is best revived by the creation of new companies, the Federal Government will take numerous measures to support new companies. We want to put a signal with this, namely a signal not of thoughtless saving pen, but of reasonable impulses. [[New applause with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] We are clear – we do not dispute this and we cannot even discuss it away – that Austria has a lot to do with technology policy in order to bring research activities in the Austrian economy to European level. This extends from the provision of education to the availability of over-company teaching workshops to the dissemination of teaching professions and from improved foreign language teaching to the strengthening of the promotion of women. And we need to take note of this and work on it. High house! Unlike many other countries, Austria has managed a major structural change in recent years. The Federal Government has set itself the goal of reducing the deficit in the Federal Budget to 2.7 percent of gross domestic product by 1997. Countries and municipalities have agreed to reduce their deficit to 0.3 percent of gross domestic product. The main reason for this policy is to preserve political scope. Tax money must not flow in excess into debt repayment, but into politically meaningful and wanted benefits. We want to save with the consolidation programme, but at the same time we must also make reforms. We see the need to save as an opportunity. In the case of large areas of expenditure in public budgets, the dynamics of staffing is reduced and administrative procedures are reduced. This common ground serves, above all, to bring about a lasting peace for all times after thousands of years of war. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] That's why the question "Yes or no to the common Europe?" No longer today, but it raises the question of the future "like each other in Europe?" [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] For a country such as Austria, which has also pursued a very successful employment policy due to its stability-oriented monetary policy, monetary union is an important prerequisite for a successful export and thus employment policy. Austria will therefore try to be with the first countries to be members of the monetary union, but also to ensure that the Social Agreement of the Maastricht Treaty is incorporated into the new Treaty in order to avoid distortion of competition by social dumping. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Without the involvement of the Central and Eastern European countries, social and environmental imbalances within the Union will become even more drastic in relation to those countries. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] High house! At a time of profound change in Europe and increasing globalisation, the Federal Government has been particularly concerned about the stability of our country and the security of our citizens. We see security as a comprehensive concept, which covers the protection of jobs, social security and the preservation of our environment, as well as issues of internal and external security or human rights and tolerance. The prospects that we want to implement, and especially in the European Union, range from closer cooperation between the European Union and the Western European Union in the areas of crisis management, peacekeeping and humanitarian operations to a policy for the enlargement of the European Union to Central and Eastern Europe, which is probably the best investment for our security. We are particularly concerned with the creation of a foreign and security policy, which can in fact be called "joint" and not just in the interest of some Member States. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Until we achieve this goal, ladies and gentlemen, it seems realistic to take a longer route. That is why it would be unwise for a country as small and geopolitical as exposed as Austria to give up security-political scope and set itself on a single institutional option. One more thing has to be said clearly: Security and stability in Europe go far beyond the structures of the European Union. The Federal Government will therefore actively develop cooperation in all security forums, thus making an important contribution to the fact that there are no new lines of separation on our continent, but a common area of peace and security. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] This applies in particular, ladies and gentlemen, to our active cooperation within the NATO "Partnership for Peace" and within the framework of the Organisation for Security and Cooperation in Europe. I would like to take this opportunity to thank all Austrians who have contributed to this very personal solidarity, who have made such an important contribution to peace in the world within the framework of IFOR in Bosnia, at the Golan, in Cyprus, in Haiti, in Cambodia and elsewhere, and who demonstrate Austria's solidarity as individuals every day. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Of course, the dynamic change in our security environment also affects the task of military national defence. The Federal Government will therefore endeavour, in addition to an efficient military defence, to increase the Federal Government's willingness to make international operations more effective. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] In the run-up to government, we have also reached an agreement, a pre-convention on civil service law. In the interests of legal certainty for civil servants, the Federal Government is committed to the final and permanent abolition of the verification of conscience and to establishing a uniform, independent duration of civil service from changing conditions. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! With regard to all the importance we attach to European integration, the Federal Government also wants to further strengthen the international presence of Austria. On the one hand, it is a question of making offensive use of the opportunities arising from free world trade for our economy and for the protection of jobs. On the other hand, we can only make full use of our design opportunities in the Union if we show a clear profile in our international relations and are willing to actively set stability and peace policy priorities. This applies to the strengthening and reform of the United Nations and to new ways of development cooperation, as well as the building of a peace order in the former Yugoslavia and a constructive partnership with Russia and Ukraine. And this also applies, and above all, to our commitment in the Middle East, where it is not only about the salvation of the peace process, but also about the fight against the terror that the community of states must face with determination – in Israel and everywhere else in the world. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] That is why the Federal Government, which I have led, will do everything in the legislative period ahead of us, so that Austria is one of the safest countries in Europe in the future. Fighting international organised crime is one of the priority objectives: Drug cartels, trafficking in human beings, towing, car sliders and the like. In the national field, it must be a matter of largely preventing the mobility of criminal organizations at the Austrian border and thus of preventing organised crime from accessing our country and through Austria to other countries, such as the European Union. The consistent extension and extension of the border service of the Gendarmerie, the continuation of the assistance of the Austrian Federal Army and the best possible cooperation of the Gendarmerie and Customs Guard are to contribute to this. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] As far as the use of modern technical tools and methods of investigation is concerned, the Federal Government is aware not only of the importance for a modern crime fight, but also of the need to protect fundamental rights. New technical investigation methods, such as acoustic and optical monitoring of persons or automated data alignment, should therefore be able to be applied in cases of a serious risk to public security and in the context of very close legal regulations relating to judicial approval. Adjustments to copyright protection, consumer protection and labour law are necessary in order to protect intellectual property and promote creativity even under new conditions. Above all, access to the new media must be as open as possible, but also cheaper and easier. By increasing global networking, by increasing capacity and faster computers, improved lines will also change the use of the media in the long term. In this field of tension, it is important for a relatively small country like Austria with its diverse culture and identity to have strong and viable media. Therefore, in the case of radio and television, we expressly confess to the side-by-side of public-law and private, commercial-oriented media. With the printing media we want to obtain the widest possible variety of daily newspapers, magazines and magazines. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] In order to ensure a strong public-law television in this situation, the ORF will be converted into a stock company and its position will be strategically secured by opening new business areas. To this end, it is also necessary to maintain the dual funding base, i.e. financing from fees and advertising. On private radio, we will innovation the Regional Radio Act in such a way that, according to the original plans in all the federal states, at least one regional and local private radios can start operations quickly. The examples of Styria and Salzburg show that the media policy approach that we had set out in the liberalisation of the radio, which was actually right, is the approach: only one regional radio per state – except for Vienna, there two – in order not to break up advertising, and in a second step only local radio. With the agreement that the ORF remains the fourth radio frequency chain, the most difficult constitutional obstacle could be removed. In the case of private commercial television, cable distribution should be regulated by law as quickly as possible in accordance with the highest judicial decisions in this field. It will also take into account the regulation of terrestrial television of the future. High house! I shall now come, Mr President, to an area which is thought to have been forgotten in the preparation of the consolidation programme, namely health care. I can assure all those who accept this that we are – quite the contrary! – a very comprehensive reform of health care. This will create the necessary conditions for ensuring the well-functioning medical care and the financing of the health system next year. The Federal Minister for Health has presented a number of reform proposals. In this context, those who blame the Federal Government for their failure to implement these structural reforms should once again be explained that these reforms cannot be decreed, but in particular must be negotiated and jointly implemented with the health care professionals who have to implement these structural reforms. In the course of the financial compensatory negotiations, the Federal Government has declared its willingness to provide additional 12 billion schilling over the next four years if the health care providers implement important structural reforms, including a binding health care plan and the introduction of performance-oriented funding. This willingness of the Federal Government, in addition to providing 12 billion schilling, is not a matter of course at a time when it is a matter of realizing a 100-billion schilling consolidation program. We therefore document the importance of the reform of Austrian health care for the Federal Government. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! I take this debate very seriously, of course, and I would remind you once again that the level of benefits for women in our country is very high. This high level is not least a result of the women's policy activities of the two previous legislative periods. Hardly any other country provides for a two-year care allowance. However, I do not know that this benefit also poses labour market problems, and I do not want to admit that we have underestimated the costs of the second year of karenz when it was introduced and therefore now have to make corrections. Additional facilities for children of all ages should therefore be provided for children of all ages with appropriate and full-day opening hours at socially graded rates. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] High house! Any presentation of social reforms and savings presupposes that the Austrian social state has reached a level similar to that of the Austrian social state. As an example of social reforms, I call for efforts, particularly through labour market policies, to increase the actual retirement age. The current special support for older workers, which was sometimes used to exempt older people at the expense of the social budgets, expires. In this context, social justice means providing for sufficient transitional provisions to ensure the life planning of those concerned. Sufficient transitional provisions are a criterion for ensuring a high level of employment. A bonus malus system is therefore introduced at the same time as the abolition of special support, which provides incentives for businesses to continue to employ older workers or to stop them in addition. In the future, a malus will have to pay for those entrepreneurs who release older workers. In order to counter the threat of an increase in unemployment, it will be necessary to use the entire tool of active labour market policy. This need will also be taken into account during the budgetary consolidation phase. In the area of unemployment insurance, the principle of saving was consistently implemented through reforms. The integration of unemployed people takes precedence over passive services. A very important issue for the future not only to protect the individual, but also to maintain our social security system is the social security of telework and other atypical work relations. In the future, workers' contracts will be included in social security in order to prevent the escape from social security. This is a very important reform measure – not only because work contracts have often been misused to cover employment relationships, but also because it is believed that atypical employment conditions will be the typical employment conditions on the labour market of the next millennium. Ladies and gentlemen! The best safeguarding of the pension is, of course, a successful economic and employment policy. Nevertheless, steps of modern development and harmonisation of systems must be taken again and again. A clear priority towards maintaining the greatest possible employment is set by reforms in the areas of incapacity for work and invalidity. The principle of rehabilitation before retirement is reinforced – including by a general period of invalidity pension for two years or for the duration of rehabilitation. The second essential aspect of pension reforms is the strengthening of the insurance principle and a new pension calculation model which favours longer periods of insurance. This will also create a financial incentive for individual workers to stay in employment longer. None of the pension reforms intervene in existing pensions. The statutory retirement age will not be increased either. Modern reforms of the pension system are without abrupt changes. However, it is certainly necessary to take greater account of the number of months of insurance. The number of required months of contribution will therefore be increased from 1. January 1997 gradually increased from 420 to 450 months. High house! I am making my statement on environmental and energy policy in relation to employment and jobs. Over many years, the achievement of the one has been seen as incompatible, as incompatible with the achievement of the other objective. It seemed that issues of environmental protection would always be considered less important when job security issues came to the fore. I mean, both views are outdated and wrong and do not correspond to today's contexts. Environmental protection does not destroy, but creates jobs. [[Congratulations on SPÖ and ÖVP as well as on the greens.]] I would like to illustrate this in two examples. Example number one: More than 3,000 jobs will be created through the granting of special tranches each of 1 billion schilling for urban water construction and the restoration of the old load. At the same time, these investments will also benefit the quality of our soils and our groundwater. Example number two: Investment in transport infrastructure not only improves the economic location, not only increases the attractiveness for industrial settlements and not only creates potential for new jobs, but also guarantees quality of life. The extension of the railway and the shift from road to rail – a key element of Austrian transport policy for many years – are therefore to be driven forward by the implementation of a rail infrastructure law. At the same time, we will work at the level of the European Union for the continuation of trans-European networks, and in particular international rail links, in order to fully exploit the ecological potential and to avoid inefficient "island solutions". However, I see only a realistic future perspective for these efforts – in particular investor advertising – if the principle of cost-truth succeeds in anchoring the principle of cost-truth more than before in our economic structure. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ, with representatives of the ÖVP and the Greens.]] Furthermore, the current discussion on the increase or introduction of road and motorway toads shows that we still have a considerable amount of conviction in this regard, both at national level and at our neighbours. The aim of our efforts must, in any event, be to achieve a rapid transfer of these systems to road pricing through the gradual increase of road tolls and charges within the meaning of the Road Costs Directive of the European Union. We have also undertaken in the field of environmental policy to strengthen efforts to protect and clean air through the use of conventional, but also new economic instruments. In this context, I would like to mention that the Federal States will receive a financial allocation of over 1 billion schilling for the implementation of environmentally friendly and energy-saving measures from the proceeds of the proposed energy levy. This corresponds, by the way, to the content and scale of the climate protection billions required by many environmental organisations. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! Today, Austria's energy policy is facing new and increased demands through its accession to the European Union. It has a good, independent reputation in Europe. This reputation is based in particular on the high share of renewable energy sources in total energy supply, i.e. hydropower, in some respects biomass, and on the diverse and committed activities of Austria against the use of nuclear energy in Europe. Both premises will also shape the work of the next four years, in particular because the serious and competent appearance of Austrian politicians and scientists at European level has for the first time brought transparency and, subsequently, thoughtfulness into the decision-making processes for the construction of nuclear power plants. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] High house! Contrary to many fears, the year 1 after Austria's accession to the European Union of domestic agriculture has not brought its downfall. Instead, agricultural income has succeeded in stabilising and opening up new opportunities at a really difficult time. Two factors were particularly responsible for this: Factor number One was the support package for agriculture, written in the German Federal Government's Europe Agreement, whose contents are jointly borne by the Federal Government, the federal states and the European Union. Factor number two – and I consider it to be at least as important – was and is the willingness of Austrian farmers to face the new challenge and – here is a comparison allowed – not to put the head in the sand, but to pack the bull with the horns. We have therefore dealt with the immediate transition to a new system relatively well – yet it must be clear to all parties that we have only taken a first step, although important. Much has to be done on the spot in the villages, in the municipalities, in the regions and in the regions, but much has also moved to the level of the European Union. In this way, we want and will take action in Brussels to ensure that the ecological orientation of agriculture becomes an integral part of the EU's common agricultural policy. To this end, it is necessary to extend the programmes for environmentally sound agriculture and forestry to the central pillar of agricultural policy and to link as much aid and support as possible to environmental support conditions. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] The second central element of home agricultural policy is the social issue. We know that many of the European Union's achievements are so designed that the large units benefit more than the smaller units. I think it must be in the interests of a farm-structured agriculture and forestry like ours to bring greater social security into the system. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] In this sense, Austria will also work to ensure that the European Union takes far more account of the social criteria in the design of agricultural support. In concrete terms, this means that, in particular in the areas of mountain and other less-favoured areas and in organic farming, there is a need to enable base amounts to be taken into account in the promotion of large farms either the effects of the cost-degression or, in the case of individual measures, the reduction of support limits. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] High house! In recent years, the Austrian tourism industry has been less and less able to exploit its capacity. This has to do with changed structural factors. Natural competitive advantages fall at the expense of the so-called "movable competitive advantages", i.e. those which are not specified by the location. However, with the shifts and changes in the structure of demand, weaknesses in the Austrian supply have also become apparent, which can now be combated at all levels. The Austrian leisure industry will always be as good as its businesses are viable, but also how well their employees are trained. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] What's this about? – In the first paper on the creation of the Austrian Institute, the aim is to create a much more flexible structure of private economy and to dispel it from the system of camera-listics. It is clear that we are taking the right path with the creation of this Austrian institute, because some other countries have acquired an excellent reputation with their institutes – I mention the Institut Français, the Goethe Institute. We believe that flexibility, greater economic efficiency, the achievement of additional revenue, the abandonment of camera lysics, that all this is much easier to achieve by disseminating than by maintaining in the system of camera lysics. That is the reason why the government has tabled this proposal, and I do not think we need to get into reminiscence of the past, we are forward-looking. We stand for this foundation of the Austrian Institute because we believe that the creation of this distributed institute, which offers German courses abroad, but may also offer additional cultural activities and events, is a better basis than the previous integration into the cultural institutions. It is by no means about these twelve planning sites, as is always claimed. We have experienced the Kasperl Theatre; You took such a thing. One thing is clear: These twelve planning posts were still available at the beginning of the year, some of which were still made for them in this year. With the establishment of the Austrian Institute, not only the planning posts, but of course the payments also fall out, and we have a complete reorganisation of this institution. If the calculations – at least for the next six years – can be performed in the individual stages – and these are realistic, non-optimistic and non-pessimistic calculations, then budget savings will be at least 4.4 million schilling within six years. I believe that we are called here in plenary, the National Council and also the Federal Government, that we can use every opportunity where savings are in. I think that is a principle to which we have to confess ourselves. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Now to the second template. This second proposal also has a breakdown on the content, namely the distribution of the Diplomatic Academy. I believe that this is an institution which has undoubtedly brought a great deal in the history of Austria for our foreign policy reputation. We all know that the predecessor, created as an oriental and later as a consular academy in 1754, did essential things, gifted from all levels of the population the possibility of targeted preparation for the higher Service of the Federal Ministry of Foreign Affairs. But it has also given many from other countries the opportunity to have such a qualified training. Let's take a look at a few figures: Since 1965, i.e. the last 30 years where the Diplomatic Academy has emerged from the Consular Academy, 619 foreigners and foreigners have completed their studies at the Diplomatic Academy. Of these 619 graduates, 330 have Austrian citizenship. Of these 330, 152 are active in the foreign service and really represent their husband or wife, they are excellently trained, so they represent the corps of what we have in higher service in the Foreign Office. It is a very, very good training, which is now to be enriched by initiatives put forward by the preparatory staff and then presented by the Foreign Minister, with even more possibilities. This gives us the opportunity to live up to the whole globalisation of what has actually happened in recent years and decades in foreign policy and training as a whole. We have taken account of the new needs, and I believe that those who will now and in the future take positions in this new distributed form of the Diplomatic Academy in Vienna are guarantees that not only the previous level will be maintained, but also that a budgetary discharge can be achieved with additional means of revenue. That is why we believe that this division of the Diplomatic Academy into a company-run public law institution with its own legal personality is the answer to the new challenges, that a reduction in the two-year course will be possible to a single one-year course, allowing the taking into account of a postgraduate higher course for international studies, thereby opening up additional revenue in the form of special courses and seminars. As far as my assessment of this bill is concerned, I can say: I believe that those who have considered this in detail not only a few months, but also in recent years have considered all the alternatives to it, have provided a good basis for this proposal to ensure that we can also be proud in the future of the divided form of the Diplomatic Academy in Vienna. They will be given more opportunities, and this increase in opportunities will also be well used by us in Austrian foreign policy. We would like to agree with these two proposals.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-03-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00010_d7e4483	Mrs Jäger is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-03-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00010_d7e4505	Mr President. Mrs. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! Today we adopt two laws, the Austrian Institute Act and the Diplomatic Academy Law, both of which have to do with Austria's relations with abroad. The Diplomatic Academy Act aims to put an institution on a new basis for many Austrian diplomats, but not only for these, an important training centre. When the then Foreign Minister Bruno Kreisky founded the Diplomatic Academy in 1964, he stated as one of his reasons for this step that talent from all levels of the population of Austria was a targeted preparation for the higher Service of the Federal Ministry of Foreign Affairs or for other international professions should be provided. This was linked to the idea that in a modern state the members of the diplomatic service would not only come from a closed circle, from a closed circle, which would always "renew" from the same families, but that all layers of the people should have access to the diplomatic service. Only such a diplomatic service is able to represent the Republic adequately abroad. In the now 30 years in which the Diplomatic Academy worked very well, about 150 later foreign officials were prepared for their profession for two years. These 150 foreign officials represent about 45 percent of Austrian graduates. Furthermore, it must not be overlooked that there have also been many graduates from other countries in the Diplomatic Academy, namely 289 from 76 countries, many of them mainly from the so-called developing countries and more recently from the former Eastern Bloc Reform States. These graduates were trained here in Austria for two years. They were able to imagine the culture of this country, of everyday life with us, and many of them have taken these links, which they could connect with Austria, into their homelands and have thus become a kind of Austrian ambassador to their homelands. Therefore, if, in the overall judgment on the activities of the Diplomatic Academy in the last three decades, a positive assessment has been made to a great extent, we must not forget that, due to changed conditions, because of the globalisation of international relations which has occurred since 1964, and also because of the increasing competition of comparable training institutions, a reform of this Diplomatic Academy is urgently necessary and correct. I think that a division of the Diplomatic Academy into a company-run public law institution with its own legal personality is much more appropriate to adapt the training offer to the needs of a modern foreign service, to the needs of students, but also to foreign interests. And for economic reasons too, such a reform is to be welcomed. However, it has now been shown that a two-year course for many potential participants, all of whom can already refer to a completed study, takes too long. Even if one does not come from good conditions, it is to a great extent important how long one still has to live without income, and also that one also has to bear training costs. For all these reasons, I support today's legislative project, which is linked to the positive traditions of the Diplomatic Academy, but also to the new circumstances. I am also confident that in a very dense and compressed year of training, it will be possible for those responsible to communicate almost the same knowledge and understanding of international relations for international problems, as was the case in the previous two-year course. However, I also think that it is important and worth mentioning that some changes and adjustments to the Government's proposal could still be made in the Committee on Foreign Affairs. In this context, I would like to point out that, at the request of MEPs Schieder and Höchtl, graduates of technical colleges have also been specifically included in the circle of those who should be prepared in the new Academy for professional activity in international organizations or in the international economy. It is certain that, at the beginning of the year, there will not be a very large number of technical colleges, but it seems to me to be important, for principle reasons, that this draft law does not lead to discrimination against graduates of higher education. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] I can imagine very well that a graduate of a university of higher education will be joining the Diplomatic Academy for another year, in order to start a career in the international economy or an interesting activity in another international field. However, I must point out in this connection that, because of the legislation given, a graduate of higher education, even though he has successfully attended the Diplomatic Academy, cannot at present come to the Austrian diplomatic service because of the fact that the conditions in other laws are not met. I think that this situation will still have to be addressed in the next few years, but I think it is far more important that this additional training opportunity has now been opened up for the fields of international economy and international organisation for high-school graduates of this goal. I would also like to point out a second point in the amendment which extends the range of training programmes offered by the Diplomatic Academy to all the authorities of the Federal Government, but also to other local authorities. It would not be possible to see why, at the request of the competent service, national staff cannot also participate in training programmes of the Diplomatic Academy. At this point, however, I would like to address a critical word to some federal states. I think it is right that servants of the federal states will be able to participate in the training programmes of the Diplomatic Academy and that two members will also come to the significantly reduced board of trustees, but I do not think it is right that only a minority of Austrian federal states will be willing to contribute to the Foundation for Scholarship Funds of the Diplomatic Academy. I think it should also be a concern for the Austrian federal states to be able to complete the Diplomatic Academy more socially weak, but very talented. I therefore appeal in this connection to the countries which currently do not want to pay any more that they will make the contributions in this regard in the future. That is why I also welcome one of those provisions in this amendment that only more members of those federal states will be established and voted in the board of trustees who contribute to the Foundation. Of course, it is also clear to me that the contributions paid by a federal state to this pot do not necessarily benefit from scholarship recipients from that state. But the funds in this pot do not have a mesh, and of course there may also be beneficiaries from other federal states, or there may also be beneficiaries from countries such as the so-called developing countries. But I think it is very small if the federal states do not want to make any more contributions for this reason. For particularly good in this bill, I consider the project that the higher courses for international studies will be set up in close connection with the University of Vienna and that cooperation with the University of Vienna will be institutionalized. This also ensures that graduates receive the designation "Master of Advanced International Studies" and otherwise a participant certificate when reaching the teaching goal. This is precisely what I think increases the attractiveness for the new Diplomatic Academy. The fact that the application in the Committee expressly refers to cooperation with the University of Vienna does not mean, however, that other universities are devalued. It is only the University of Vienna of the universities in question that has an excellent offer to be presented in all the fields of training in the diplomatic academy. In addition, the undisputed world reputation of the University of Vienna will certainly be attractive and enjoyable for the future graduates. It has been discussed several times today: A second law is being passed here today, namely the Austrian Institute Act. It is intended to continue a positive tradition under changed conditions with greater efficiency and also with the necessary sparingness. The Austrian German courses abroad are intended by an Austrian Institute G.m.b.H. I would like to thank the rapporteur, Mr President, for his excellent report and for his excellent report. Ladies and gentlemen! International networking in all areas is increasing. Austria's external relations are becoming ever closer and more important. The legislator is constantly required that our country and its representatives find the appropriate framework conditions to meet these new challenges in the best possible way. I hope – yes, I am confident – that the new Diplomatic Academy Act has given those responsible an instrument to achieve the objectives of training efficiently and as best as possible. I believe that we have also created conditions for the fact that future graduates are not only well trained, but also more understanding of the various international relations, of the various problems of the international community, and thus also of getting a weapon to help reduce these problems. Thank you.

In the course of its deliberations, the Committee has made the following statement on its decision-making practice: "In the case of the private acquittals of §§ 111 (absolute renunciation), 113 (punishment of a criminal offence already committed), 115 (injury) and 152 (indemnity) StGB, the extraditional desire of the courts is agreed by the Immunity Committee. The Immunity Committee will hold a general debate before the 30th century. June 1998 to analyse the experiences gained and to shape its practice accordingly." The Immunity Committee, as a result of its deliberations, proposes that the National Council: 1. In response to the request of the State Court of Criminal Matters in Vienna of 17 November, the Court of Justice of the European Communities referred to the Court of Justice for a preliminary ruling a question for a preliminary ruling a question on the interpretation of Council Regulation (EEC) No. January 1996, 9dE VR 13555/95, Hv 7930/95, for approval of the official persecution of the MP to the National Council Dr. Jörg Haiderwill be referred to in Article 57. 3 B-VG notes that there is a link between the criminal act alleged by the private prosecutor and the parliament's political activity as National Councillor Dr Jörg Haiderbe. 2. An official persecution of the MP to the National Council Dr. Jörg Haiderwill be approved. I also report on 53 of the additions to the Stenographical Protocols, namely two requests from the International District Court for approval of the official persecution of the Member of the National Council Dr Jörg Haider. In the course of its deliberations, the Committee adopted the following conclusions by a large majority: "The difference between the two requests lies in the basis of the prosecution, namely in the first case the private contribution, in the second case the petition of the prosecutor, which was made responsible for the persecution on the basis of an authorisation of the private prosecutor. The additional provision of § 117 StGB concerns only the question of a conditions of persecution without changing the facts underlying the procedure. On the basis of the term ‘punitive action’ in Article 57(1). 3 B-VG states that the Immunities Committee has to decide on extradition for a particular subject, but not on a specific legal qualification. Accordingly, it is not important for the Immunities Committee whether the basis of the criminal proceedings is a private contribution or the authorisation of the injured person in his rights, on the basis of which the public prosecutor enters under § 117 StGB." ParlaMint-AT_1996-03-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00010_d7e5608	We are now going into the debate on urgent Question 268/J, which Mrs Petrovic has addressed to the Federal Minister for Science, Research and Art concerning savings measures at universities and students. ParlaMint-AT_1996-03-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00010_d7e5772	I now give the floor to this urgent question by Mrs Dr. Petrovic.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-03-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00010_d7e5793	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! We have been in Austria for years now, taking note of not only I but, as I believe, many of us with great concern, namely the increase in a somewhat anti-education and cultural climate, which can be seen in the attempt to polarize. Again and again it says: Students live at the expense of the general public! It gives the impression that students are dealing with non-students and non-students who lead a sunny life, while others have to pursue a hard bread job. This climate of polarization poses a great danger to a country, especially a country which claims to be a high level of education, with high demands on science and culture, and which must be a country with high quality standards and a correspondingly high level of education in terms of economics. This happened already in the time of the Hainburger Au, when it was tried to bring workers and students against each other; and all attempts to contribute to social cohesion were repeatedly countercared by opposing tendencies. It is you, Mr Federal Minister, who have never contributed to such trends in the past. On the contrary: We have all remembered you as someone who is trying to act in the sense of the climate of openness, tolerance and social cohesion. I find it all the more sad and sad that a load package is now to be carried out in your area of business, just as no other group in society can be achieved in a similar form. There are massive pressures from the savings package both in the area of students and in particular in the field of secondary education and universities as a whole. As has been said, no other population group is required. For students, this burden is, for example, a dramatic restriction on family allowances, which will affect 95 percent of students. This means more than 20,000 s less per family with students per year. In addition, there is a loss of the associated services, i.e. a loss of other transfers: The removal of free travel and therefore a restriction on student mobility. In the future, this will be followed by the buying-in of retirement periods which will go into the hundreds of thousands of schilling for those affected, especially for women who have only recently been able to reach the equal treatment package as a compromise after long negotiations. For women, this means that they are seeking higher qualifications and that the equal treatment package has been removed by cold means. A woman who is in training longer than 171⁄2 years of age can no longer benefit from the statutory retirement age according to the equal treatment package. These are massive discrimination and discrimination among the discriminated against! New hierarchies are also introduced, e.g. in the field of secondary university construction, which is affected by dramatic salary losses, at least from about 14 percent, usually, especially in the mass fields, from more than 33 percent to 40 percent. This population group is supposed to lose more than a third of its income! And the way in which it is done is really only more than scandalous! ==References== In February, this draft law was passed to the university with a term of opinion up to 4/5. ==References== Three or four days of the week have remained to say that a population group should lose more than one third of its income! They have gone into the media on the part of the Federal Government and have spoken of savings among other groups, of zero-one rounds in the civil service, of raising railway pensions by only 1 or 2 percentage points. They have presented this to the public as a major savings measure and are famous for it. However, this group accounts for 30 and 40 percent of income with a growing burden. You know as well as I do, Federal Minister, that the burdens of universities – and I really mean universities, students, medium-sized buildings, also professorship – have risen steadily. I say: Thanks be to God they have partly risen, because thanks to God the Austrian universities were largely open. Thank God, however, has been known in the past as a principle of free access to higher education and has also enabled students from socially less rosy shifts to study quite satisfactorily. There was still much to improve, because the scholarships were certainly not cost-effective, and there were still strong preferences from students from better-earning shifts with well-paid parents. There were still discriminations, but instead of fighting these discriminations, you are now causing massive deterioration for all. This is an answer that can only be pointed out in sharpest terms. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] It is not a question of intelligent savings, you have not even tried to accept the offer of universities to make their own contribution in the context of necessary savings measures. The universities were ready to save, and no one assumes that it would not be possible to save even at the universities. We all know that, as a result of the one-year budgeting, there is often almost a pressure to spend higher expenditure in November or December when money is left. It is true that books for libraries will then be bought on a case-by-case basis. It is certainly also nice to have money for it, but in this area it would be possible to save to a certain extent. And the universities offered you that. They would have reached out to the Federal Government. They were willing, in their own autonomy, which on other occasions is repeatedly conjured to make these savings and to examine for themselves where savings measures are possible. We would have been able to determine, of course, where savings are possible at universities – in other words, students, medium-sized, professors and professors. We would have accepted this. But against this approach almost an attack command and against this scrapping action, as you carry out it in a unilateral way, which I consider to be an attack on the education system in Austria, we will do massively and fully support the protests of students and middle-class construction. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] There are absolutely impossible requirements. It is no longer possible for most studies to study at the minimum study time. Some of the events are not offered at all. Additional obstacles were introduced due to a lack of staff and material resources of the universities. Pro-seminars and similar events have been introduced so that the operation can be handled at all. You know as well as I do that it has become simple and simply impossible in most areas of study to complete one semester per study period over the minimum duration, especially where there are large numbers of students and students. They therefore impose financial sanctions on the failure to achieve an objective which has become absolutely unattainable. They ask the people concerned to do something impossible. In other words, you are not taking a sparing measure, but a fiscal measure, which leads to a real scrapping of the people concerned and, unfortunately, to the strengthening of this anti-educational trend outlined by me at the outset – what do we need it for? What do they need to study for? – and this very, very negative and absolutely against the future of Austria. Ladies and gentlemen! However, they do not only introduce the most massive strains at all in the hope that these groups, the students and the university middle-level building have too little sympathy in the population and that the media and the large majority of the population will not participate in the protests. In the future, the hierarchy is: Professor, assistant with habilitation – that is, lecturer, lecturer – then those who have acquired a doctorate and are assistants, and finally those who have "only" – under quotation marks – the magisterium, otherwise sufficient for all possible state qualifications, for ministerial offices and highest state dignity by far.
